# Source map

Cochrane (2023), *The Fiscal Theory of the Price Level*, Chapter 19, "Monetarism" (pp. 433–464),
with the precise claims of Chapter 11 (pp. 315–318) and §14.1 (p. 342), and of Online Appendix
§1.6 "Money in continuous time" (pp. 567–569), Chapter 3 "Pruning multiple monetary equilibria"
(§§3.1–3.2.4, pp. 612–632) and Chapter 4 "Money in continuous time" (pp. 635–641). Each formal
claim is listed with its Lean name, relative to the namespace `Cochrane.Monetarism`. Page numbers
are book pages; "A1", "A3" and "A4" are Online Appendix Chapters 1, 3 and 4. The book has no
labelled propositions, so rows are keyed by equation number, figure, footnote, the survey's names
for the precise claims of the verbal chapters (V1–V5, V11, NI-7) or page. Rows follow Chapter 19
section by section. The Chapter 11 and §14.1 claims follow §19.1, whose module proves them, and
the Online Appendix Chapter 3 material follows §19.4, which defers to it. Then come Online
Appendix §1.6 and Chapter 4, the links between modules with Galí's money-rule determinacy
theorem and its `κ → ∞` limit, the household problem on a genuine probability space (first with
the regularity hypotheses, then without them, with the expectation systems on countable state
spaces and the null-state counterexamples), the copied Blanchard–Kahn theorem, the copied
measure-theoretic core and the shared tools. Active fiscal policy across all paths, and under
uncertainty on event trees and on a probability space, follows §19.5. Woodford's sections on
convexity, Taylor-rule transversality and Obstfeld–Rogoff follow his monetary-targeting dynamics.

The linear and present-value results hold for an arbitrary `ExpectationSystem`, so under perfect
foresight and under uncertainty; date-`t` quantities are known at `t`, and results that take them
out of `E_t` assume `ExpectationSystem.PullOut`. The nonlinear phase dynamics of money in utility,
the Online Appendix Chapter 3 equilibria and the cash-in-advance equilibria are perfect-foresight,
as in the book; the first-order conditions are also derived on a finite event tree. The
measure-theoretic modules (`HouseholdMeasure` onward) pose the household problems on a probability
space with a filtration, where `E_t` is conditional expectation, information may grow forever and
equations hold almost surely; there the first-order conditions, (19.15)–(19.16), (19.31)–(19.32)
for every `V`, Fact 2 and the necessity of the transversality condition are derived from
optimality. The `TidyR6Household…` modules drop the bounds on prices and plans and the global
smoothness of utility, and characterise both household optima exactly. Continuous time is
deterministic except in `TidyR6StochasticDrift`, which gives the `E_td(·)` forms of Online
Appendix §1.6 their content under uncertainty; the Hamiltonian conditions of Online Appendix
Chapter 4 are proved necessary and sufficient (`TidyR6Pontryagin` onward). Survey error codes E13–E23 are those of the survey of Chapters 11–19
(`notes/survey_cochrane_ch11-19.md`); see [corrections](corrections.md). Rows marked "restated"
reproduce results of the Woodford Chapter 2 project or of other Cochrane projects in this
namespace.

## Restated from the Woodford Chapter 2 project: convexity, Taylor-rule transversality and Obstfeld–Rogoff, pp. 441–445, and A3 §3.2.2, pp. 621–625 (TidyR6WoodfordSections)

The three sections of Woodford (2003), Chapter 2, §4, that `MoneyTargetingDynamics` left out,
restated from that project's `MoneyTargetingDynamics` and `GlobalDynamics` modules with two
renamings (its `e7_counterexample` is `e7_convexity_fails`, and its `IsORPath` is `IsORMapPath`,
since this project's `ObstfeldRogoff.IsORPath` is a different notion), then linked to
`MoneyTargetingDynamics`. Perfect foresight.
E4 and E7 are errors of Woodford's book, recorded in the Woodford Chapter 2 project.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Woodford (2003) p. 130 (E7); Cochrane §19.3.2, pp. 442–445 | with `v′(m) = e^{−m}`, `c = 1`, `F` is positive, strictly increasing and tends to `0` at `0⁺`, but is not convex (restated) | `TidyR6WoodfordSections.vpE7`, `TidyR6WoodfordSections.e7_convexity_fails` |

## §19.5.5 Active fiscal policy across all paths, pp. 453–454 (TidyR5ActiveFiscal)

Perfect foresight, constant money growth `M_{t−1} = M_{−1}(1 + µ)ᵗ` with `1 + µ > β`, and the
constant active surplus `s = (b(1 − β) − µy/V)/(1 + µ)` of the standard path; an equilibrium is
any `CashInAdvance.PF.MonetaryEq`, with any bond path. See [corrections](corrections.md) for the
p. 454 proviso.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cochrane p. 454 (tool) | every equilibrium bond price is at least the standard one, `Q_t ≥ β/(1 + µ)` | `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.bondPrice_ge` |
| Cochrane pp. 453–454 | with `B_{−1} + M_{−1} ≥ 0` (`bV + y ≥ 0`) every equilibrium, late switches to the zero bound and partial deflations included, has `P_t = M_tV/y` and the standard bonds; exactly one equilibrium | `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.active_fiscal_price_unique`, `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.active_fiscal_existsUnique` |
| Cochrane pp. 453–454 (counterexample) | with `B_{−1} + M_{−1} < 0` the zero-rate deflation `P_t = βᵗP_0`, `P_0 = (B_{−1} + M_{−1})(1 − β)/s < M_0V/y`, is a second equilibrium | `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.active_fiscal_two_equilibria` |
| Cochrane p. 454, corrected by p. 453 | **the price level is unique across all paths iff `B_{−1} + M_{−1} ≥ 0`**; the book's case `b ≥ 0`, extending `CashInAdvance.PF.active_fiscal_prunes` to all paths | `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.active_fiscal_unique_iff`, `TidyR5ActiveFiscal.active_fiscal_prunes_all` |

## §19.5.5 Active fiscal policy under uncertainty, pp. 448–454: event trees and every `ExpectationSystem` with full support (TidyR6ActiveFiscalTree, TidyR6ActiveFiscalExist)

Constant endowment `y` and velocity `V`, money an arbitrary positive adapted process, surpluses
an arbitrary adapted process, for any `ExpectationSystem` with the pull-out property and full
support (`TidyR6ActiveFiscalTree.FullSupport`), in particular every finite event tree. An
equilibrium (`TidyR6ActiveFiscalTree.StochMonetaryEq`) imposes the conditional transversality
conditions at every date and state. See [corrections](corrections.md) for the p. 454 conditions.

## §19.5.5 Active fiscal policy under uncertainty on a probability space, pp. 448–454 (TidyR6ActiveFiscalMeasure, TidyR6ActiveFiscalMeasureExist, TidyR6ActiveFiscalBubble)

The same model on a probability space with a filtration (`ExpectationsMeasure`), every condition
and conclusion almost surely. An equilibrium (`TidyR6ActiveFiscalMeasure.MeasMonetaryEq`) imposes
the transversality condition only in unconditional expectation, `E[β^Tx_T] → 0`; see
[corrections](corrections.md).

## Online Appendix §1.6 under uncertainty: the `E_td(·)` forms (1.32)–(1.34), p. 567 (TidyR6StochasticDrift)

`ContinuousTime` restates the deterministic forms. Here `E_td(·)` is given the content that can
be made precise without stochastic integrals: exact conditional increments on a `Δ`-grid for any
`ExpectationSystem` with the pull-out property, and in continuous time a drift notion on a
probability space with a filtration indexed by `ℝ`. The product step with a martingale part of
`Λ/P` is proved in the `TidyR7ProductStep…` modules below, for adapted continuous
bounded-variation bonds.

| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 (1.32), p. 567 | `E_td(Λ/P)/(Λ/P) = −i dt` as the limit of the discrete conditional increment `−i/(1 + iΔ)` | `TidyR6StochasticDrift.rate_increment`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.rate_increment_tendsto` |
| A1 (1.32), (1.34), p. 567 | the exact conditional increments of `n_kB_k` and `n_k(B_k + M_k)` on the `Δ`-grid, and the recursion they give | `TidyR6StochasticDrift.incr_1_32`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.incr_1_34`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.rec_of_incr_1_32` |
| A1 (1.33)–(1.34), p. 567 | on the `Δ`-grid, valuation iff the conditional transversality condition (from `CashInAdvance.Stochastic.pv_iff_tvc`) | `TidyR6StochasticDrift.valuation_1_33`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.valuation_1_34` |
| A1 (1.32), p. 567 | `E_tdX = a dt` defined as the martingale property of `X − ∫a`; a process with `C¹` paths has its pathwise derivative as drift | `TidyR6StochasticDrift.HasDrift`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.hasDrift_of_pathwise` |
| A1 (1.32) ⇒ (1.33), (1.34), p. 567 | integrating the expected increment, `Y_s = E_s[Y_T] + E_s∫_s^Tg`; valuation `Y_s = E_s∫_s^∞g` iff `E_s[Y_T] → 0` in `L¹` | `TidyR6StochasticDrift.valuation_of_drift`, `TidyR6StochasticDrift.valuation_iff_tvc` |

## Online Appendix §1.6: pathwise Riemann–Stieltjes tools for the product step of (1.32), p. 567 (TidyR7StieltjesIntegral, TidyR7StieltjesSums, TidyR7StieltjesCalculus, TidyR7StieltjesVariation)

The product step of (1.32), `Λ/P · dB + E_t d(Λ/P)·B = E_t d(ΛB/P)`, is to be proved path by path
from the exact partition identity for products and limits of Riemann–Stieltjes sums, without a
stochastic integral. These modules are the deterministic tools: the integral against Mathlib's
Lebesgue–Stieltjes vector measure `BoundedVariationOn.vectorMeasure`, the convergence of the sums,
integration by parts, and measurability in a parameter. The product step itself is proved in the
next section.

## Online Appendix §1.6: the product step of (1.32) in expectation form, p. 567 (TidyR7CondDominated, TidyR7ProductStep, TidyR7ProductStepCases, TidyR7ProductStepCoin)

The middle step of (1.32), `Λ/P · dB + E_t d(Λ/P)·B = E_t d(ΛB/P)`, proved in integrated,
expectation form without a stochastic integral, for `B` adapted with continuous bounded-variation
paths and `Λ/P = A + M` (`A` càdlàg of bounded variation, `M` a càdlàg martingale), under
domination conditions. Bonds that jump with `Λ/P` are treated in the section on jumping bonds
below; `B` with a diffusive part is treated, for Itô processes, in the last section on
(1.32) with diffusive bonds; a general martingale part is not covered (see the README).

## Online Appendix §1.6 under uncertainty: the chain (1.31) → (1.32) → (1.33)–(1.34), pp. 567–568 (TidyR7MonetarismAC, TidyR7MonetarismModel, TidyR7MonetarismValuation, TidyR7MonetarismCoin, TidyR7MonetarismLinks, TidyR7MonetarismCoinInf, TidyR7MonetarismUI)

The whole chain of §1.6 on a probability space with a filtration indexed by `ℝ`: `Λ, P > 0`
adapted, `Λ/P = A + N` with `N` a càdlàg martingale (jumps allowed) and `dA = −i(Λ/P) dt`, bonds
and money adapted, money with continuous bounded-variation paths, the flow (1.31) path by path,
and a general interest rate on money `i^m`. No measurability of `i`, `i^m`, `s` in `ω` is
assumed. The product step of the previous section gives (1.32); integrating it gives the
finite-horizon budget, and three readings of "integrate to infinity" give (1.33)–(1.34). Money
and bonds that jump with `Λ/P` are treated in the next section, and diffusive (Itô) bonds and
money in the last section on (1.32) with diffusive bonds; bonds with a general martingale
part are not covered (see the README).

| Book | Claim | Lean |

## Online Appendix §1.6: bonds and money that jump with `Λ/P`, (1.32)–(1.34), pp. 567–568 (TidyR7JumpSums, TidyR7ProductStepJump, TidyR7ProductStepJumpCoin, TidyR7MonetarismJump, TidyR7MonetarismJumpCoin)

The product step of the previous sections assumes bonds with continuous paths. Here `B` is
adapted, càdlàg and of bounded variation, and may jump together with `X = Λ/P`. The step then
needs a third term, the jump covariation `Σ_{s<u≤t} ΔX_uΔB_u`, or, equivalently, the
Lebesgue–Stieltjes integral of the current (post-jump) `X` against `dB`. No stochastic integral
is needed. In the monetary model money jumps at news dates (an open-market operation) while
`B + M` stays continuous, so `ΔB = −ΔM`: line 3 of (1.32) and (1.33) hold verbatim with the
post-jump Lebesgue–Stieltjes integral `∫ n dM`, the pre-jump reading `∫ n_− dM` misses
`E_t[ΔMΔ(Λ/P)]`, and (1.34) is unaffected. Bonds with a diffusive part are in the next five
sections: the Brownian infrastructure, the Itô integral and the product rule they need, and
(1.32)–(1.34) with diffusive bonds in the last.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 §1.6, p. 567 (tool for (1.32)) | a càdlàg function equals its right limit; the frozen function of a càdlàg `g` has right limit `g(x)` on `[a, b]`; a càdlàg integrator does not charge the left endpoint, so `∫_a^b f dg = ∫_{(a,b]} f dg` and the integral depends on `f` only on `(a, b]` | `TidyR7JumpSums.rightLim_eq_of_isCadlag`, `TidyR7JumpSums.rightLim_clampFn_eq`, `TidyR7JumpSums.variation_stMeasure_left`, `TidyR7JumpSums.rsInt_eq_Ioc`, `TidyR7JumpSums.rsInt_congr_Ioc` |
| A1 §1.6, p. 567 (tool) | left limits: `f(x−) = f(x)` on `(a, b]` for `f` continuous on `[a, b]`, so then `∫ f_− dg = ∫ f dg`; the left limits of a càdlàg function are measurable, bounded by a bound of `f` on `(c, x)`, and integrable against every finite-variation measure on `[a, b]` | `TidyR7JumpSums.leftLim_eq_of_continuousOn`, `TidyR7JumpSums.rsInt_leftLim_of_continuousOn`, `TidyR7JumpSums.measurable_leftLim`, `TidyR7JumpSums.abs_leftLim_le`, `TidyR7JumpSums.rsIntegrable_leftLim` |

## Online Appendix §1.6: Brownian infrastructure for (1.32) with diffusive bonds, pp. 567–568 (TidyR7BrownianSchauder, TidyR7BrownianUnit, TidyR7BrownianExist, TidyR7BrownianTools)

A bond or discount factor with a diffusive component needs a Brownian integrator. Mathlib defines
`ProbabilityTheory.IsBrownianReal` (index `ℝ≥0`) but proves no existence theorem. These modules
supply one, with the filtration, martingale and moment tools that the quadratic variation and the
Itô integral will need; the quadratic variation is in the next section, the Itô integral in
the one after, the product rule in the one after that, and (1.32)–(1.34) with diffusive bonds
in the last. The Lévy–Ciesielski construction on `[0,1]` (`TidyR7BrownianSchauder`,
all of it, and the part of `TidyR7BrownianUnit` that the extension uses) is restated, proofs kept,
from the LeanDynamicProgramming project's Meyn–Tweedie `LimitTheorems` modules `LevyCiesielski`
and `BrownianMotion` (namespace `MeynTweedie.LimitTheorems` there); each docstring names its
origin. The motion on `[0, ∞)` is the time change `W(t) = (1+t)B(t/(1+t)) − tB(1)` of the
`[0,1]` motion `B`, that is `(1+t)` times the Brownian bridge at `t/(1+t)`, so one Gaussian
sequence suffices and every path is continuous.

## Online Appendix §1.6: quadratic variation and infinite variation of Brownian motion, pp. 567–568 (TidyR7BrownianQV, TidyR7BrownianVariation)

The rule `(dW)² = dt` and its consequences for (1.32) with diffusive bonds. `W` is any
pre-Brownian motion with measurable values, read on the index `ℝ` of the product step through
`TidyR7BrownianTools.bmReal`; partitions are Track 1's `TidyR7StieltjesSums.Partition s t` with
`0 ≤ s`. Conditional and weighted results hold for any admissible filtration
(`TidyR7BrownianQV.BMFiltration`: `bmReal W` adapted, `𝓕_u` independent of every later increment),
of which the natural filtration is one. The quadratic variation `Σ(ΔW_i)² → t − s` holds in `L²`,
in probability, almost surely along partitions with summable meshes (so along the dyadic ones),
and conditionally on `𝓕_s`; so Brownian paths have infinite variation almost surely, and the
bounded-variation product step of `TidyR7ProductStep` provably cannot take a Brownian bond. The
cross sum of `W` with a continuous bounded-variation path tends to `0` path by path. The stochastic
integral is in the next section.

## Online Appendix §1.6: the Itô integral `∫ H dW` for (1.32) with diffusive bonds, pp. 567–568 (TidyR7ItoL2, TidyR7ItoSums, TidyR7ItoIntegral, TidyR7ItoBridge, TidyR7ItoContinuity)

The stochastic integral that (1.32) with diffusive bonds needs. `W` is any pre-Brownian motion
with measurable values, read on `ℝ` through `TidyR7BrownianTools.bmReal`, `𝓕` any admissible
filtration (`TidyR7BrownianQV.BMFiltration`), partitions are Track 1's
`TidyR7StieltjesSums.Partition s t` with `0 ≤ s`. An admissible integrand
(`TidyR7ItoIntegral.ItoIntegrand`) is adapted, bounded and has almost surely continuous paths on
`[s, t]`. The integral `TidyR7ItoIntegral.itoInt` is a chosen adapted mean-square limit of the
left-point sums along the dyadic partitions (`0` if there is none, and for `t ≤ s`); the
definition needs no hypothesis, so it also gives `∫ W dW`. It is the limit along any partitions
with mesh `→ 0`, satisfies the Itô isometry, is linear, is a martingale in `t` with the conditional
isometry, has a continuous modification on `[s, T]`, agrees with Track 1's pathwise integral for
bounded-variation integrands, and gives `∫_s^t W dW = (W_t² − W_s² − (t − s))/2`. Unbounded
integrands, Itô processes and the product rule with covariation are in the next section.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 §1.6, pp. 567–568 (the mean-square limit) | **`X k → Y` in `L²(P)`**: all square integrable and `E[(X k − Y)²] → 0`; then `‖X k − Y‖_{L²} → 0`; `E[f²] = ‖f‖²_{L²}` and `‖f‖_{L²} = √E[f²]` | `TidyR7ItoL2.L2Tendsto`, `TidyR7ItoL2.L2Tendsto.tendsto_eLpNorm`, `TidyR7ItoL2.integral_sq_eq_toReal_eLpNorm_sq`, `TidyR7ItoL2.eLpNorm_eq_ofReal_sqrt` |

## Online Appendix §1.6: Itô processes and the product rule `d(XY) = X dY + Y dX + d[X, Y]` for (1.32) with diffusive bonds, pp. 567–568 (TidyR7ItoUnbounded, TidyR7ItoProducts, TidyR7ItoProcess, TidyR7ItoCovariation, TidyR7ItoProductRule)

The product rule that (1.32) with diffusive bonds needs. The book's step
`E_t d(ΛB/P) = Λ/P·dB + B·E_t d(Λ/P)` has no covariation term; when both factors diffuse the
bracket `d[X, Y] = HK dt` appears. The integral of the previous section is extended, with the
SAME hypothesis-free `TidyR7ItoIntegral.itoInt`, to mean-square continuous integrands
(`TidyR7ItoUnbounded.MSIntegrand`: adapted, square integrable at every time, uniformly continuous
in mean square, no bound and no path regularity), which contain the bounded admissible integrands,
Itô processes, and products `X·K` of one with a bounded admissible `K`. An Itô process
(`TidyR7ItoProcess.ItoProcess`) is `X_u = X_s + ∫_s^u a dr + ∫_s^u H dW` a.s. at each `u`, with
`X_s` square integrable and `a`, `H` bounded admissible; all statements are at fixed times, with
no continuous version. `∫ X dY := ∫ X b du + ∫ X K dW`, the time integral in mean square
(`TidyR7ItoProducts.dtInt`, the pathwise one when the paths are a.s. continuous). The product rule
holds almost surely, conditionally on `𝓕_s` and in the expected-increment form; the bracket-free
book form holds iff `E_s[∫_s^t HK du] = 0`, and fails for `X = Y = W`. The general product step
in the book's economy, and so (1.32)–(1.34) with diffusive bonds, are in the next section.

## Online Appendix §1.6: (1.32)–(1.34) with diffusive bonds, pp. 567–568 (TidyR7ItoBondStep, TidyR7MonetarismIto, TidyR7MonetarismItoValuation, TidyR7MonetarismItoCex, TidyR7MonetarismItoCexInf)

The product rule of the previous section applied to the book's economy. Bonds `B` (drift `β`,
diffusion `σ_B`) and `X = Λ/P` (drift `ν`, diffusion `σ_X`) are Itô processes on the same `W`
(`TidyR7ItoProcess.ItoProcess`, bounded adapted almost surely continuous coefficients). The step
from the middle line of (1.32) to its last line is then
`E_t d(BX) = X dB + B E_t dX + E_t d[B, X]` with `d[B, X] = σ_Bσ_X dt`; the book's bracket-free
step holds iff `E_s[∫_s^t σ_Bσ_X du] = 0`, and against every Itô `Λ/P` iff bonds have no
diffusion. In the model `TidyR7MonetarismIto.ItoMonetaryModel` (`P > 0`, `n = Λ/P` with drift
`−in`, the flow (1.31) on the coefficients, so that `σ_M = −σ_B` and `B + M` is locally
riskless) line 1 of (1.32) holds as a rate identity and line 2 verbatim, line 3 gains the
bracket, (1.33) gains `−∫σ_Xσ_B du` in the flow, and (1.34) holds verbatim. Three economies on
the constructed motion `TidyR7BrownianExist.bmPath` show that the printed line 3 fails when bonds' diffusion is
correlated with `Λ/P`, that it holds with both diffusive when the diffusions are active on
disjoint intervals, and that the printed (1.33) fails on `[0, ∞)` although the transversality
condition holds. Their `Λ/P = 1 + W` and `1 + ∫φ dW` can be negative; positive analogues,
with `Λ/P ∈ [1, 3]`, are in the section on Itô's formula below.

| --- | --- | --- |

## Online Appendix §1.6: the sharp condition for the printed (1.33)–(1.34), with infinite-horizon and dominated instances, pp. 567–568 (TidyR8MonetarismUISharp, TidyR8MonetarismItoDom, TidyR8MonetarismItoDomInf, TidyR8MonetarismMoneyInf, TidyR8MonetarismJumpInf)

The printed `E_s∫_s^∞` forms of (1.33) and (1.34) were proved under domination or `L¹`
convergence of the flows, and `TidyR7MonetarismUI.ui_headline` shows that some such hypothesis is
needed. Here the condition is characterised: for finite-horizon budgets
`Y_s = E_s[Y_T] + E_s[F_T]` whose flows converge almost surely to `G` and are bounded below by an
integrable `−h`, the printed form holds iff the flows converge in `L¹` (under the `L¹`
transversality condition), equivalently are uniformly integrable, and iff they converge
conditionally in `L¹` (under the almost-sure one); without the lower bound "printed ⇒ `L¹`"
fails. Three economies follow: (1.34) on `[0, ∞)` with nonzero money and interest on money, the
pre-jump (1.33) failing on `[0, ∞)` while the transversality condition holds, and the dominated
(1.33) with the bracket for diffusive bonds, with its instance on economy 3 of the previous
section. The lower-bound counterexample is a generic budget system on `ℕ`, not a
`TidyR7MonetarismModel.MonetaryModel` economy (see the README).

## Online Appendix §1.6: Itô's formula and a positive diffusive `Λ/P` for (1.32)–(1.34), pp. 567–568 (TidyR9ExpMartingale, TidyR9ItoTaylor, TidyR9ItoPathSums, TidyR9ItoWeightedBracket, TidyR9ItoFormula, TidyR9ItoFormulaVariants, TidyR9MonetarismItoPositive, TidyR9MonetarismItoPositiveInf)

The counterexample economies of the previous sections use `Λ/P = 1 + W` and `1 + ∫φ dW`, which
can be negative. A positive diffusive `Λ/P` is a function of an Itô process, and its drift and
diffusion come from Itô's formula. The formula is proved here for `f(t, X)`, along the exact
partition identity and four limits (the time sum, the Itô sum, the weighted bracket and the
Taylor remainder); `X` is an Itô process with almost surely continuous paths (a hypothesis, since
`TidyR7ItoProcess.ItoProcess` is specified at fixed times), `f_t` and `f_xx` are bounded, and the
remainder is controlled by uniform continuity on the compact range of each path. Its variants
give `f(X)`, `f(u, X_u)`, discounting, `1/X` and `exp X` as Itô processes. With
`Λ/P = 2 + sin W ∈ [1, 3]` and `P = 1` the bond-pricing drift `−i(Λ/P)` forces a random adapted
nominal rate `i`, and the counterexamples to the printed (1.32) and the infinite-horizon (1.33),
and the disjoint-diffusion instance, hold with `Λ/P > 0` throughout. The lognormal
`exp(σW − σ²t/2)` has an unbounded diffusion coefficient and is not an Itô process here; only its
martingale property is proved.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 §1.6, pp. 567–568 (Itô's formula, the space and time steps) | Taylor with a second-order remainder: `\|f''(z) − f''(x)\| ≤ c` between `x` and `y` gives `\|f y − f x − f'(x)(y − x) − ½f''(x)(y − x)²\| ≤ c(y − x)²`; the first-order bound in time, `\|g v − g u − g'(u)(v − u)\| ≤ c(v − u)` if `\|g' − g'(u)\| ≤ c` on `[u, v]`; a derivative bounded by `L` gives an `L`-Lipschitz function | `TidyR9ItoTaylor.abs_taylor2_le`, `TidyR9ItoTaylor.abs_taylor1_le`, `TidyR9ItoTaylor.abs_sub_le_of_deriv_bound` |
| A1 §1.6, pp. 567–568 (**the uniform pathwise estimate**) | for `f(u, x)` with `f_t`, `f_xx` jointly continuous and a path continuous on `[s, t]`, for every `ε > 0` there is `δ > 0` such that for `u ≤ v` in `[s, t]` with `v − u < δ` the time error is `≤ ε(v − u)`, the shift of `f_t` is `≤ ε` and the space remainder is `≤ ε(x_v − x_u)²`; uniform continuity is used only on the compact range of the path | `TidyR9ItoTaylor.exists_delta_taylor_path` |

## Online Appendix §1.6: Doob's inequality, adapted continuous versions of `∫ G dW` and the product step with `M = W` or `M = ∫ K dW` for (1.32), pp. 567–568 (TidyR10DoobDiscrete, TidyR10DoobContinuous, TidyR10DoobIto, TidyR10DoobVersion, TidyR10DoobAugment, TidyR10DoobProductStep, TidyR10DoobFubini, TidyR10DoobMSVersion)

Mathlib v4.34 has only the weak-type maximal inequality and no completion of a filtration. Doob's
`L²` inequality is proved here from scratch, first on finite grids (from orthogonality of future
increments to the square-integrable past, with no finite measure assumed), then on dyadic grids
and in continuous time for any modification with almost surely right-continuous paths. With it
the Itô integral gets one version on `[s, ∞)` adapted to `𝓕` itself with almost every path
continuous, and one with EVERY path continuous adapted to the `P`-completed filtration; for the
latter the conditional expectations and the Itô integral are those of `𝓕`. This supplies what
`TidyR7ProductStep.ProductStepData` needs of a Brownian or Itô-integral martingale part: every
path càdlàg and an integrable `pathSup·Var`. The conclusion of the product step is stated back
on `𝓕_s`. Bonds are adapted, bounded, with continuous bounded-variation paths and a measurable
bounded variation (assumed); the instances use `B_u = u`.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 §1.6, pp. 567–568 (Doob, the running maximum) | `S_l = max_{k ≤ l}\|Y_k\|` and its increments `D_0 = S_0`, `D_l = S_l − S_{l−1}`; `S_0 = \|Y_0\|`, `S_{l+1} = max(\|Y_{l+1}\|, S_l)`, `\|Y_k\| ≤ S_l` for `k ≤ l`, `S_l ≥ 0`, non-decreasing, `≤ Σ_{k ≤ l}\|Y_k\|`; `D_0 = \|Y_0\|`, `D_{l+1} = S_{l+1} − S_l`, `D_l ≥ 0`, `Σ_{l ≤ N} D_l = S_N` | `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMaxStep`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax_zero`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax_succ`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.le_runMax`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax_nonneg`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax_le_succ`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMax_le_sum`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMaxStep_zero`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMaxStep_succ`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.runMaxStep_nonneg`, `TidyR10DoobDiscrete.sum_runMaxStep` |

## Online Appendix §1.6: two Brownian motions and the (1.32) product step with bonds and `Λ/P` on different shocks, pp. 567–568 (TidyR10TwoBMExist, TidyR10TwoBMCross, TidyR10TwoBMIto, TidyR10TwoBMBondStep, TidyR10TwoBMCorr)

I5e's bracket `σ_Bσ_X dt` is for bonds and `Λ/P` on one Brownian motion. Here two independent
Brownian motions are built on one probability space with one filtration admissible for both, the
rule `dW¹dW² = 0` is proved, and bonds and `Λ/P` are Itô processes with two drivers. The
step from the middle line of (1.32) to its last line then carries the bracket
`σ_B¹σ_X¹ + σ_B²σ_X²`; it vanishes when bonds and `Λ/P` load on different independent shocks,
however diffusive both are, and with shocks of correlation `ρ` the printed step fails on every
`[s, t]` iff `ρc ≠ 0`. Brackets of Itô processes are in `L¹`; the money–bond model
`TidyR7MonetarismIto.ItoMonetaryModel` is not restated with two drivers.

## Online Appendix §1.6: the (1.32) product step with jumps and diffusion together, its money form and an instance on a Brownian motion with an independent coin, pp. 567–568 (TidyR11JumpDiffStep, TidyR11JumpDiffCoinBM, TidyR11JumpDiffCoinBMStep, TidyR11JumpDiffMoney)

I4's jump step (`TidyR7ProductStepJump.JumpStepData.jump_step`) has bonds and `Λ/P` that jump
together but do not diffuse; I5e's bond step (`TidyR7ItoBondStep.bond_step`) has bonds and `Λ/P`
that diffuse on one Brownian motion but do not jump. Here both are present at once:
`X = Λ/P = A + M_d + M_c` and `B = B_bv + B_c`, with `A`, `B_bv` adapted, càdlàg and of bounded
variation, `M_d` a càdlàg bounded-variation martingale, and `M_c = ∫K dW`, `B_c = ∫σ_B dW`
driftless Itô processes with every path continuous. The step from the middle line of (1.32) to
its last line carries the whole covariation `[B, X]`, continuous part `σ_BK dt` and jump part
`ΔBΔX`, and the book's two-term step holds iff its conditional expectation vanishes; the two
parts can cancel. The proof splits `BX = B_bvX + AB_c + M_dB_c + B_cM_c` and applies I4's jump
step to the first three products and I5d's conditional product rule
(`TidyR7ItoProductRule.condExp_product_rule`) to the last. On the pure Brownian filtration every
martingale is continuous, so the instance adds a fair coin, independent of `W`, revealed at time
`1`. The valuation equations (1.33)–(1.34) are not restated for jump-diffusion bonds.

## Online Appendix §1.6: stochastic differential equations, Itô processes with unbounded coefficients, geometric Brownian motion and a positive diffusive `Λ/P` with a known rate for (1.31)–(1.32), pp. 567–568 (TidyR12SDETools, TidyR12SDEProcess, TidyR12SDEPicard, TidyR12SDEExist, TidyR12SDEGauss, TidyR12SDEGBM, TidyR12SDEBracket, TidyR12SDEProductRule, TidyR12SDEMonetary, TidyR12SDEMonetaryGBM, TidyR12SDEVersion)

§1.6 specifies `Λ/P`, bonds and money by their stochastic differentials. I5d's Itô processes
(`TidyR7ItoProcess.ItoProcess`) have bounded coefficients; B1's positive diffusive `Λ/P`,
`2 + sin W`, came with a random rate, and the lognormal discount factor, whose diffusion `σn` is
unbounded, was outside the class. Here Itô processes
with mean-square continuous coefficients (`TidyR12SDEProcess.ItoProcessMS`) contain the bounded
ones; stochastic differential equations `dX = b(t, X) dt + σ(t, X) dW` with Lipschitz
coefficients have a unique mean-square continuous solution, by Picard iteration, with an adapted
almost surely continuous version; geometric Brownian motion is that solution for linear
coefficients, proved directly from the left-point sums; the bracket and the conditional product
rule extend to `X` with unbounded coefficients against bounded `Y`; and in the money–bond model
with such a `Λ/P` the GBM economy `Λ/P = n₀e^{−iu}exp(σW_u − σ²u/2)` has a constant known rate
`i`, and the printed line 3 of (1.32) fails on every `[s, t]`. Uniqueness is among mean-square
continuous solutions; there is one Brownian motion and one dimension; Itô's formula, the almost
sure product rule and (1.33)–(1.34) are not redone for the wider class.

## Online Appendix §1.6: the martingale representation theorem on the Brownian filtration, the extended Itô integral and the martingales the corrected (1.32) step covers, pp. 567–568 (TidyR13MRepIntegral, TidyR13MRepCutoff, TidyR13MRepOneStep, TidyR13MRepDensity, TidyR13MRepTheorem, TidyR13MRepMonetarism)

§1.6 writes `Λ/P`, bonds and money as diffusions driven by one Brownian motion. On the Brownian
filtration this loses no martingale: every square-integrable martingale is `M_0 + ∫ H dW`. The
representing integrand is not known to be mean-square continuous, so the Itô integral is first extended
to the `L²(dt × dP)`-closure of the jointly measurable mean-square continuous integrands, where it
agrees with `TidyR7ItoIntegral.itoInt` and keeps the isometry, linearity and the martingale
property. Integrands switched on over a sub-interval, which jump, lie in the class.
A square-integrable variable measurable with respect to `σ(W_r : 0 ≤ r ≤ T)` and
orthogonal to all Itô integrals is zero (trigonometric exponential martingales, characteristic
functions and the π-λ theorem); the representation follows by orthogonal projection onto a closed
subspace of `L²(P)` and is unique. A represented martingale is a zero-drift
`TidyR12SDEProcess.ItoProcessMS`, the form `TidyR12SDEMonetary.ItoMonetaryModelMS` needs for `Λ/P`,
iff its integrand has a mean-square continuous version. The horizon is finite, `W` has every path
continuous, the filtration lies in `σ(W_r : r ≤ T)` (the augmented filtration is not treated), and
it is not shown that every progressively measurable square-integrable integrand is in the class.

## Online Appendix Chapter 4, §4.1.3: Pontryagin necessity and sufficiency for (4.16)–(4.17), pp. 640–641 (TidyR6PontryaginTools, TidyR6Pontryagin, TidyR6PontryaginPC, TidyR6PontryaginPCSuff, TidyR6Halkin)

The book's problem stated precisely: maximise `∫_0^∞ e^{−δt}(u(c_t) + v(m_t)) dt` subject to
(4.16), with `u, v` concave and `C¹` on `(0, ∞)`. `ContinuousTime` proves the consequences of the
Hamiltonian conditions; here they are proved necessary and sufficient, first for continuous and
then for piecewise-continuous optimal consumption.

## Further links: finite-time explosion, the tree household problem, and Galí's money rule (LinksTidy)

Three bridges left open by `Links`. Galí's (2015) money-rule system (34) is restated, with its row
equivalence and equilibrium notion; Galí's sticky-price determinacy theorem for it is proved in
`TidyR5MoneyRule`, in the next section.

## Galí's money rule: determinacy and the flexible-price limit, §19.1, p. 435; §19.2, pp. 436–437 (TidyR5MoneyRule, TidyR5KappaLimit)

Galí's (2015) determinacy theorem for (34), restated from the Galí Chapter 4 project (its modules
MoneyRuleSystem, MoneyRuleRoots and MoneyRuleDeterminacy) with the namespace renamed, for the
system and equilibrium notion of `LinksTidy.GaliMoneyRule`, which are not copied. The proof uses
the general Blanchard–Kahn theorem `TidyR5BK`. The docstrings of the restated block cite Galí
(2015), as `LinksTidy.GaliMoneyRule` does.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Galí (2015), p. 75 (restated) | the characteristic cubic `f(z) = det(A_{M,1} − zA_{M,0})`; `det A_{M,0} = 1 + ση`, `det A_{M,1} = σηβ`; `A_M = A_{M,0}⁻¹A_{M,1}`; its eigenvalues are the roots of `f` | `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM1_sub_smul_AM0`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM0`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM1`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.AM`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM_sub_smul`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charpoly_AM_eval`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.isRoot_charpoly_AM_iff` |
| Galí (2015), p. 75 (restated) | the cubic expanded; `f(0) = σηβ`, `f(1) = κ`, negative leading coefficient; a real root above one; `det A_M = σηβ/(1 + ση) ∈ (0, 1)` | `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_expand`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_zero`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_one`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_signs`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_root_gt_one`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.AM_has_eigenvalue_gt_one`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.det_AM_pos_lt_one` |
| Galí (2015), p. 75, fn. 14 (restated) | the book's numerically supported claim: `f = −(1 + ση)(z − r)(z² + q₁z + q₀)` with `r > 1` and a Schur-stable quadratic factor; the coefficients, the factorisation, `f(−1)`, a root above one for `σ, η ≥ 0`, `κ > 0`, `0 ≤ β ≤ 1`, the values of the quadratic and the Schur–Cohn conditions; **the claim holds**, also on Galí's domain | `TidyR5MoneyRule.BookDeterminacyClaim`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.coef2`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.coef1`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_eq_coef`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quadQ1`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quadQ0`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quadQ1_mul`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quadQ0_mul`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_factor`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.charCubic_neg_one`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.exists_root_gt_one`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quad_values`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.quad_schurStable`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.bookDeterminacyClaim_holds`, `TidyR5MoneyRule.bookDeterminacyClaim_gali` |

## §§19.3.1 and 19.5.1 The household problem on a probability space, pp. 439–453 (HouseholdMeasure)

The household problem common to money in utility and cash in advance, on a probability space with
a filtration (`ExpectationsMeasure.MeasExpectationSystem`), with complete contingent-claim markets
at the state price `Λ_t` (p. 439). Statements hold almost surely unless marked exact.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cochrane (19.9), p. 439 | expected discounted utility `E∑βᵗu_t` as a genuine integral of an absolutely convergent series; Fubini for series; uniformly bounded utility processes are admissible; changing a plan at finitely many dates | `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible`, `HouseholdMeasure.expU`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.summable`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.integrable`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.integrable_sum`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.hasSum`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.of_bdd`, `HouseholdMeasure.UAdmissible.modify`, `HouseholdMeasure.aestronglyMeasurable_tsum`, `HouseholdMeasure.hasSum_of_eq_off_finset` |

## §19.5 The cash-in-advance household on a probability space, pp. 447–453 (CashInAdvanceMeasure)

every velocity `V`, with the wedge `w_t = 1 + (1/V − 1)ι_t`. Statements hold almost surely.

## (19.11)–(19.12), p. 439, and (19.28), p. 449: conditional present values and the necessity of the transversality condition (HouseholdMeasureTVC)

The Kamihigashi deviation is restated and adapted from the Cochrane `IntertemporalModel` project
(its `HouseholdMeasureTVC`), generalised to complete markets and a bundle direction `(1, ϑ)`.

## §§19.3.1 and 19.5.1 The household problem without regularity hypotheses: deviations and differentiation, pp. 439–441 (TidyR6HouseholdRoll, TidyR6HouseholdLocal)

The tools behind the first-order conditions and the transversality condition of the next three
sections, which drop the regularity hypotheses of `HouseholdMeasure`: prices are only `PriceWeak`
(no bounds on `Λ`, `P` or their reciprocals), the plan is unbounded, and utility is `C¹` on an
open domain instead of all of `ℝ²`. Statements hold almost surely unless marked exact.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |

| Cochrane p. 439; (19.12), p. 439; (19.15), p. 441 | consequences of weak regularity: `pk_t > 0`, `pk_tP_t = Λ_t`; `pk_t`, `Q_t` and `ι_t` are known at `t`; `E_t[pk_{t+1}] = Q_tpk_t`; `Λ_tι_t = Λ_t − P_tE_t[pk_{t+1}]` | `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.pk_pos`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.pk_mul_P`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.sm_pk`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.sm_bondPrice`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.sm_iota`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.E_pk_eq`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.PriceWeak.lam_iota` |

| Cochrane footnote 1, p. 440 | homogeneous deviations (the flow constraint with zero endowment, taxes and initial money, and an eventually constant wealth value `D`); **adding `ε` times one to a feasible plan keeps it feasible as soon as the wealth value shifted by `εD` satisfies the no-Ponzi condition**; homogeneous deviations add | `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.HomDev`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.feasible_add`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.HomDev.add` |
| Cochrane footnote 1, p. 440; (19.12), p. 439 | the perpetual rollover: consume `z` and hold `ζ` more at `t`, issue `−P_tζ + X/pk_{t+1}` and roll `X/pk_n` over for ever, `X = −Λ_t(z + ι_tζ)`; **it is a homogeneous deviation whose date-0 debt value stays `E[X]`**, so two rollovers at different dates with cancelling values are budget-neutral | `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.rollX`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.rollC`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.rollA`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.moneyIn_rollC`, `TidyR6HouseholdRoll.roll_homDev` |

## §19.5, pp. 447–453: the cash-in-advance optimum characterised by the Karush–Kuhn–Tucker conditions (TidyR6HouseholdCIA, TidyR6HouseholdCIAKKT)

Utility `u(c)` is `C¹` on an open set `I` of consumption levels (e.g. `(0, ∞)`) and prices are
only `PriceWeak`. No bound on consumption, money or prices, no `Q_t ≤ 1` and no lower bound on
overnight money is assumed.

| Cochrane Ch. 2, p. 29; (19.9), p. 439 | functions that ignore the state: expectation, integral, integrability, boundedness; `∑ 1` diverges | `TidyR6HouseholdTVCCx.E_fun_const`, `TidyR6HouseholdTVCCx.integral_fun_const`, `TidyR6HouseholdTVCCx.integrable_fun_const`, `TidyR6HouseholdTVCCx.bddAt_fun_const`, `TidyR6HouseholdTVCCx.not_summable_one` |

## Ch. 2, pp. 29–30, and (19.15)–(19.16), (19.31)–(19.32): expectation systems, null states and the pointwise first-order conditions (TidyR6HouseholdRep, TidyR6HouseholdNull)

| Book | Claim | Lean |

| Cochrane Ch. 2, p. 29 | a positive linear functional on `Ω → ℝ` charges only finitely many states; on a countable `Ω` it vanishes off them; **on a countable `Ω` a positive unital linear functional is a finitely supported average** | `TidyR6HouseholdRep.support_finite`, `TidyR6HouseholdRep.vanish_off_support`, `TidyR6HouseholdRep.functional_rep` |
| Cochrane Ch. 2, pp. 29–30; p. 439 | **every `ExpectationSystem` on a countable `Ω` is given by finitely supported conditional probabilities**, `E_tf(ω₀) = ∑_{ω∈F} p_ωf(ω)`, using only positivity, linearity and `E_t1 = 1` | `TidyR6HouseholdRep.expectation_rep` |
| Cochrane Ch. 2, pp. 29–30; (19.9), p. 439 | an expectation system on two states whose date-0 expectation ignores the null state `false`; its axioms and the pull-out property; **the date-0 expectation is blind to the null state**, so optimality cannot restrict prices or plans there | `TidyR6HouseholdNull.evalTrue`, `TidyR6HouseholdNull.nullSystem`, `TidyR6HouseholdNull.nullSystem_E0`, `TidyR6HouseholdNull.nullSystem_E_succ`, `TidyR6HouseholdNull.nullSystem_pullOut`, `TidyR6HouseholdNull.E0_blind` |

## The general Blanchard–Kahn theorem (TidyR5BK)

Galí's determinacy theorem (`TidyR5MoneyRule`) needs the general Blanchard–Kahn theorem.
`TidyR5BK` is a verbatim copy of the Cochrane `StickyPriceFiscal` project's `BKCopy`, itself a
verbatim copy of the Woodford Chapter 4 project's `InvariantSplit`, `GeneralBKForward` and
`GeneralBK` modules nested as sub-namespaces: the same 129 declarations in the same order, only
the namespace changed. The two projects' core `Expectations` modules are identical, so no
adaptation was needed. Its docstrings keep the Woodford (2003) App. C.1 citations; the module
docstring cites Cochrane App. §1.9, pp. 573–576, and says how Chapter 19 uses it.

| Book | Claim | Lean |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Woodford (2003) App. C.1, p. 670 (tools) | complex eigenvalues of a real matrix, the stable and unstable counts, no unit eigenvalue; eigenvectors, powers, the number of eigenvalues; geometric decay of powers for a stable matrix and its converse | `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.eigs`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.stableCount`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.NoUnit`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.mem_eigs_iff`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.exists_eigvec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.exists_left_eigvec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.mem_eigs_of_eigvec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.pow_mulVec_eigvec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.map_ofReal_pow`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.card_eigs`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.pow_entry_bound`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.norm_le_one_of_pow_bounded`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.unstableCount`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.stableCount_add_unstableCount` |
| Woodford (2003) App. C.1, p. 670 | the bounded-orbit subspace and its complex version; generalised eigenvectors of stable eigenvalues have bounded orbits | `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.boundedOrbits`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.mem_boundedOrbits`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.mulVec_mem_boundedOrbits`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.mem_boundedOrbits_of_orbit`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.re_mulVec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.im_mulVec`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.boundedOrbitsC`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.maxGenEigenspace_le_boundedOrbitsC`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.finrank_boundedOrbitsC_le`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.finrank_finset_sup`, `TidyR5BK.InvariantSplit.card_filter_eq_sum` |

exact.
